					<h1 id="firstHeading" class="firstHeading mw-first-heading"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Benjamin Harrison</span></h1>
							
<div id="p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown mw-portlet mw-portlet-lang"  >
	<input type="checkbox" id="p-lang-btn-checkbox" role="button" aria-haspopup="true" data-event-name="ui.dropdown-p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown-checkbox mw-interlanguage-selector" aria-label="Go to an article in another language. Available in 118 languages"   >
	<label id="p-lang-btn-label" for="p-lang-btn-checkbox" class="vector-dropdown-label cdx-button cdx-button--fake-button cdx-button--fake-button--enabled cdx-button--weight-quiet cdx-button--action-progressive mw-portlet-lang-heading-118" aria-hidden="true"  ><span class="vector-icon mw-ui-icon-language-progressive mw-ui-icon-wikimedia-language-progressive"></span>

<span class="vector-dropdown-label-text">118 languages</span>
	</label>
	<div class="vector-dropdown-content">

		<div class="vector-menu-content">

					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">President of the United States from 1889 to 1893</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">This article is about the president of the United States. For other people with the same name, see <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="Benjamin Harrison (disambiguation)">Benjamin Harrison (disambiguation)</a>.</div>

<p><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="Seal of the President of the United States" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Seal_of_the_President_of_the_United_States.svg/70px-Seal_of_the_President_of_the_United_States.svg.png" decoding="async" width="70" height="70" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Seal_of_the_President_of_the_United_States.svg/105px-Seal_of_the_President_of_the_United_States.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Seal_of_the_President_of_the_United_States.svg/140px-Seal_of_the_President_of_the_United_States.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="2424" data-file-height="2425" /></span></span>

<span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/List_of_personal_coats_of_arms_of_presidents_of_the_United_States" title="List of personal coats of arms of presidents of the United States"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Coat_of_Arms_of_William_Henry_Harrison_and_Benjamin_Harrison.svg/40px-Coat_of_Arms_of_William_Henry_Harrison_and_Benjamin_Harrison.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="47" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Coat_of_Arms_of_William_Henry_Harrison_and_Benjamin_Harrison.svg/60px-Coat_of_Arms_of_William_Henry_Harrison_and_Benjamin_Harrison.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Coat_of_Arms_of_William_Henry_Harrison_and_Benjamin_Harrison.svg/80px-Coat_of_Arms_of_William_Henry_Harrison_and_Benjamin_Harrison.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="395" data-file-height="462" /></a></span></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Benjamin_Harrison_series" title="Template:Benjamin Harrison series"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Benjamin_Harrison_series" title="Template talk:Benjamin Harrison series"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Benjamin_Harrison_series&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Benjamin Harrison</b> (August 20, 1833&#160;&#8211;&#32;March 13, 1901) was an American politician who served as the 23rd <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_United_States" title="President of the United States">president of the United States</a> from 1889 to 1893. He was a member of the <a href="/wiki/Harrison_family_of_Virginia" title="Harrison family of Virginia">Harrison family of Virginia</a>—a grandson of the ninth president, <a href="/wiki/William_Henry_Harrison" title="William Henry Harrison">William Henry Harrison</a>, and a great-grandson of <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison_V" title="Benjamin Harrison V">Benjamin Harrison&#160;V</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States" title="Founding Fathers of the United States">Founding Father</a>.
</p><p>Harrison was born on a farm by the <a href="/wiki/Ohio_River" title="Ohio River">Ohio River</a> and graduated from <a href="/wiki/Miami_University" title="Miami University">Miami University</a> in Oxford, Ohio. After moving to <a href="/wiki/Indianapolis" title="Indianapolis">Indianapolis</a>, he established himself as a prominent local attorney, Presbyterian church leader, and politician in <a href="/wiki/Indiana" title="Indiana">Indiana</a>. During the <a href="/wiki/American_Civil_War" title="American Civil War">American Civil War</a>, he served in the <a href="/wiki/Union_Army" title="Union Army">Union Army</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Colonel_(United_States)" title="Colonel (United States)">colonel</a>, and was confirmed by the <a href="/wiki/U.S._Senate" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Senate">U.S. Senate</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Brevet_(military)" title="Brevet (military)">brevet</a> <a href="/wiki/Brigadier_general_(United_States)" title="Brigadier general (United States)">brigadier general</a> of volunteers in 1865. Harrison unsuccessfully ran for <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Indiana" title="Governor of Indiana">governor of Indiana</a> in 1876. The <a href="/wiki/Indiana_General_Assembly" title="Indiana General Assembly">Indiana General Assembly</a> elected Harrison to a six-year term in the Senate, where he served from 1881 to 1887.
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Republican_Party_(United_States)" title="History of the Republican Party (United States)">Republican</a>, Harrison was elected to the presidency in <a href="/wiki/1888_United_States_presidential_election" title="1888 United States presidential election">1888</a>, defeating the <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Democratic_Party_(United_States)" title="History of the Democratic Party (United States)">Democratic</a> incumbent <a href="/wiki/Grover_Cleveland" title="Grover Cleveland">Grover Cleveland</a> in the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Electoral_College" title="United States Electoral College">Electoral College</a> despite losing the popular vote. Hallmarks of Harrison's administration were unprecedented economic legislation, including the <a href="/wiki/McKinley_Tariff" title="McKinley Tariff">McKinley Tariff</a>, which imposed historic protective trade rates, and the <a href="/wiki/Sherman_Antitrust_Act" title="Sherman Antitrust Act">Sherman Antitrust Act</a>. Harrison also facilitated the creation of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_National_Forest" class="mw-redirect" title="United States National Forest">national forest reserves</a> through an amendment to the Land Revision Act of 1891. During his administration six western states were admitted to the Union. In addition, Harrison substantially strengthened and modernized the <a href="/wiki/U.S._Navy" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Navy">U.S. Navy</a> and conducted an active foreign policy, but his proposals to secure federal education funding as well as voting rights enforcement for African Americans were unsuccessful.
</p><p>Due in large part to surplus revenues from the tariffs, federal spending reached one billion dollars for the first time during his term. The spending issue in part led to the defeat of the Republicans in the <a href="/wiki/1890_United_States_House_election" class="mw-redirect" title="1890 United States House election">1890 midterm elections</a>. Cleveland defeated Harrison for reelection in <a href="/wiki/1892_United_States_presidential_election" title="1892 United States presidential election">1892</a>, due to the growing unpopularity of high tariffs and high federal spending. He returned to private life and his law practice in Indianapolis. In 1899 he represented <a href="/wiki/United_States_of_Venezuela" title="United States of Venezuela">Venezuela</a> in its <a href="/wiki/British_Guiana" title="British Guiana">British Guiana</a> boundary dispute with <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland" title="United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland">Great Britain</a>. Harrison traveled to the court in Paris as part of the case and after a brief stay returned to Indianapolis. He died at his home in Indianapolis in 1901 of complications from <a href="/wiki/Influenza" title="Influenza">influenza</a>. Many have praised Harrison's commitment to African Americans' voting rights, and his work ethic and integrity, but scholars and historians generally <a href="/wiki/Historical_rankings_of_presidents_of_the_United_States" title="Historical rankings of presidents of the United States">rank him in the bottom half</a> among U.S. presidents.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Family_and_education">Family and education</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:JSHarrison.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/JSHarrison.jpg/110px-JSHarrison.jpg" decoding="async" width="110" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/JSHarrison.jpg/165px-JSHarrison.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/JSHarrison.jpg/220px-JSHarrison.jpg 2x" data-file-width="250" data-file-height="380" /></a><figcaption>John Scott Harrison</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Benjamin_Harrison_Birth_Site.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Benjamin_Harrison_Birth_Site.jpg/220px-Benjamin_Harrison_Birth_Site.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Benjamin_Harrison_Birth_Site.jpg/330px-Benjamin_Harrison_Birth_Site.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Benjamin_Harrison_Birth_Site.jpg/440px-Benjamin_Harrison_Birth_Site.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a><figcaption>Birthplace marker in <a href="/wiki/North_Bend,_Ohio" title="North Bend, Ohio">North Bend, Ohio</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Harrison was born on August 20, 1833, in <a href="/wiki/North_Bend,_Ohio" title="North Bend, Ohio">North Bend, Ohio</a>, the second of Elizabeth Ramsey (Irwin) and <a href="/wiki/John_Scott_Harrison" title="John Scott Harrison">John Scott Harrison</a>'s ten children. His ancestors included immigrant Benjamin Harrison, who arrived in <a href="/wiki/Jamestown,_Virginia" title="Jamestown, Virginia">Jamestown, Virginia</a>, circa 1630 from <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">England</a>. Harrison was of entirely English ancestry, all of his ancestors having emigrated to America during the early colonial period.<sup id="cite_ref-Bruce_Stanard_1894_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bruce_Stanard_1894-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison was a grandson of U.S. President <a href="/wiki/William_Henry_Harrison" title="William Henry Harrison">William Henry Harrison</a> and a great-grandson of <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison_V" title="Benjamin Harrison V">Benjamin Harrison V</a>, a Virginia planter who signed the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Declaration_of_Independence" title="United States Declaration of Independence">Declaration of Independence</a> and succeeded <a href="/wiki/List_of_governors_of_Virginia" title="List of governors of Virginia">Thomas Nelson Jr.</a> as <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Virginia" title="Governor of Virginia">governor of Virginia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun20057–8_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun20057–8-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBarnhartRiker1971315_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarnhartRiker1971315-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOwens20076_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOwens20076-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-VIII_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VIII-5">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison was seven years old when his grandfather was elected U.S. president, but he did not attend <a href="/wiki/Inauguration_of_William_Henry_Harrison" title="Inauguration of William Henry Harrison">the inauguration</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun20058_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun20058-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> His family was distinguished, but his parents were not wealthy. John Scott Harrison, a two-term <a href="/wiki/United_State_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="United State Congress">U.S. congressman</a> from <a href="/wiki/Ohio" title="Ohio">Ohio</a>, spent much of his farm income on his children's education.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun20059Sievers1968v._1,_pp._21–23_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun20059Sievers1968v._1,_pp._21–23-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBoomhower49_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoomhower49-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Despite the family's modest resources, Harrison's boyhood was enjoyable, much of it spent outdoors fishing or hunting.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESievers196822–23v._1_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESievers196822–23v._1-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison's early schooling took place in a log cabin near his home,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200510_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200510-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> but his parents later arranged for a tutor to help him with college preparatory studies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESievers196824–29v._1_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESievers196824–29v._1-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Fourteen-year-old Benjamin and his older brother, Irwin, enrolled in <a href="/wiki/Farmer%27s_College" class="mw-redirect" title="Farmer&#39;s College">Farmer's College</a> near <a href="/wiki/Cincinnati" title="Cincinnati">Cincinnati</a>, Ohio, in 1847.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESievers196829–30v._1_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESievers196829–30v._1-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> He attended the college for two years<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace188853_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace188853-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Farmer&#39;s_College_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Farmer&#39;s_College-14">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> and while there met his future wife, <a href="/wiki/Caroline_Harrison" title="Caroline Harrison">Caroline "Carrie" Lavinia Scott</a>, a daughter of <a href="/wiki/John_Witherspoon_Scott" title="John Witherspoon Scott">John Witherspoon Scott</a>, the school's science professor, who was also a Presbyterian minister.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200511,_15_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200511,_15-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison transferred to <a href="/wiki/Miami_University" title="Miami University">Miami University</a> in <a href="/wiki/Oxford,_Ohio" title="Oxford, Ohio">Oxford, Ohio</a>, in 1850, and graduated in 1852.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200621–23_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200621–23-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESievers1968v._1,_p._58_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESievers1968v._1,_p._58-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> He joined the <a href="/wiki/Phi_Delta_Theta" title="Phi Delta Theta">Phi Delta Theta</a> fraternity, which he used as a network for much of his life. He was also a member of <a href="/wiki/Delta_Chi" title="Delta Chi">Delta Chi</a>, a law fraternity that permitted dual membership.<sup id="cite_ref-fraternities_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fraternities-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Classmates included <a href="/wiki/John_Alexander_Anderson" title="John Alexander Anderson">John Alexander Anderson</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200523_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200523-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> who became a six-term U.S. congressman, and <a href="/wiki/Whitelaw_Reid" title="Whitelaw Reid">Whitelaw Reid</a>, Harrison's vice presidential running mate in 1892. At Miami, Harrison was strongly influenced by history and political economy professor <a href="/wiki/Robert_Hamilton_Bishop" title="Robert Hamilton Bishop">Robert Hamilton Bishop</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200510–11Sievers1968v._1,_pp._31–34_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200510–11Sievers1968v._1,_pp._31–34-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> He also joined a <a href="/wiki/Presbyterian" class="mw-redirect" title="Presbyterian">Presbyterian</a> church at college and, like his mother, became a lifelong Presbyterian.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace188858_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace188858-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Marriage_and_early_career">Marriage and early career</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Benjamin_Harrison_c1850.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Benjamin_Harrison_c1850.jpg/170px-Benjamin_Harrison_c1850.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="207" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Benjamin_Harrison_c1850.jpg/255px-Benjamin_Harrison_c1850.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Benjamin_Harrison_c1850.jpg/340px-Benjamin_Harrison_c1850.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1831" data-file-height="2232" /></a><figcaption>Benjamin Harrison c. 1850</figcaption></figure>
<p>After his college graduation in 1852, Harrison studied law with Judge <a href="/wiki/Bellamy_Storer_(1796%E2%80%931875)" class="mw-redirect" title="Bellamy Storer (1796–1875)">Bellamy Storer</a> of <a href="/wiki/Cincinnati,_Ohio" class="mw-redirect" title="Cincinnati, Ohio">Cincinnati</a>, but before he completed his studies, he returned to Oxford, Ohio, to marry Caroline Scott<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200511–12,_15–15,_23_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200511–12,_15–15,_23-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> on October 20, 1853. Caroline's father, a Presbyterian minister, performed the ceremony.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200523_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200523-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The Harrisons had two children, <a href="/wiki/Russell_Benjamin_Harrison" title="Russell Benjamin Harrison">Russell Benjamin Harrison</a> (August 12, 1854&#160;– December 13, 1936) and <a href="/wiki/Mary_Harrison_McKee" title="Mary Harrison McKee">Mary "Mamie" Scott Harrison</a> (April 3, 1858&#160;– October 28, 1930).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200527,_29_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200527,_29-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison and his wife returned to live at The Point, his father's farm in southwestern Ohio, while he finished his law studies. Harrison was admitted to the Ohio bar in early 1854,<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> the same year he sold property that he had inherited after the death of an aunt for $800 (equivalent to $26,056&#32;in 2022), and used the funds to move with Caroline to <a href="/wiki/Indianapolis" title="Indianapolis">Indianapolis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Indiana" title="Indiana">Indiana</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200526_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200526-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Harrison began practicing law in the office of John H. Ray in 1854 and became a <a href="/wiki/Town_crier" title="Town crier">crier</a> for the federal court in Indianapolis, for which he was paid $2.50 per day.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200527,_29_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200527,_29-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> He also served as a Commissioner for the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Claims" title="United States Court of Claims">U.S. Court of Claims</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECowen_et_al.92_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECowen_et_al.92-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Harrison became a founding member and first president of both the University Club, a private <a href="/wiki/Gentlemen%27s_club" title="Gentlemen&#39;s club">gentlemen's club</a> in Indianapolis, and the Phi Delta Theta Alumni Club.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200522_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200522-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Harrison and his wife became members and assumed leadership positions at Indianapolis's First Presbyterian Church.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter7_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter7-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Having grown up in a <a href="/wiki/Whig_party_(United_States)" class="mw-redirect" title="Whig party (United States)">Whig</a> household, Harrison initially favored that party's politics, but joined the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Republican_Party" class="mw-redirect" title="United States Republican Party">Republican Party</a> shortly after its formation in 1856 and campaigned on behalf of Republican presidential candidate <a href="/wiki/John_C._Fr%C3%A9mont" title="John C. Frémont">John C. Frémont</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200518_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200518-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> In 1857 Harrison was elected Indianapolis city attorney, a position that paid an annual salary of $400 (equivalent to $12,563&#32;in 2022).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200629_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200629-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBoomhower50_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoomhower50-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1858, Harrison entered into a law partnership with William Wallace to form the law office of Wallace and Harrison.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200528Sievers1968v._1,_p._105_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200528Sievers1968v._1,_p._105-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> In 1860, he was elected <a href="/wiki/Reporter_of_decisions" title="Reporter of decisions">reporter</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Indiana_Supreme_Court" title="Indiana Supreme Court">Indiana Supreme Court</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBoomhower50_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoomhower50-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Harrison was an active supporter of the Republican Party's platform and served as Republican State Committee's secretary. After Wallace, his law partner, was elected county clerk in 1860, Harrison established a new firm with William Fishback, Fishback and Harrison. The new partners worked together until Harrison entered the <a href="/wiki/Union_Army" title="Union Army">Union Army</a> after the start of the <a href="/wiki/American_Civil_War" title="American Civil War">American Civil War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESievers1968171v._1_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESievers1968171v._1-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Civil_War">Civil War</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:General_Benjamin_Harrison--%22Come_on_boys!%22_-_Battle_of_Resaca_-_May_13th_to_16th_1864.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/c0/General_Benjamin_Harrison--%22Come_on_boys%21%22_-_Battle_of_Resaca_-_May_13th_to_16th_1864.jpg/170px-General_Benjamin_Harrison--%22Come_on_boys%21%22_-_Battle_of_Resaca_-_May_13th_to_16th_1864.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="228" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/c0/General_Benjamin_Harrison--%22Come_on_boys%21%22_-_Battle_of_Resaca_-_May_13th_to_16th_1864.jpg/255px-General_Benjamin_Harrison--%22Come_on_boys%21%22_-_Battle_of_Resaca_-_May_13th_to_16th_1864.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/c0/General_Benjamin_Harrison--%22Come_on_boys%21%22_-_Battle_of_Resaca_-_May_13th_to_16th_1864.jpg/340px-General_Benjamin_Harrison--%22Come_on_boys%21%22_-_Battle_of_Resaca_-_May_13th_to_16th_1864.jpg 2x" data-file-width="719" data-file-height="965" /></a><figcaption>Harrison during the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Resaca" title="Battle of Resaca">Battle of Resaca</a> leading the <a href="/wiki/70th_Indiana_Infantry_Regiment" title="70th Indiana Infantry Regiment">70th Indiana</a>, May 1864; Harrison was a colonel at the time.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:General-Benjamin-Harrison-in-uniform,-1865.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/General-Benjamin-Harrison-in-uniform%2C-1865.jpg/170px-General-Benjamin-Harrison-in-uniform%2C-1865.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="231" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/General-Benjamin-Harrison-in-uniform%2C-1865.jpg/255px-General-Benjamin-Harrison-in-uniform%2C-1865.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/General-Benjamin-Harrison-in-uniform%2C-1865.jpg/340px-General-Benjamin-Harrison-in-uniform%2C-1865.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1301" data-file-height="1769" /></a><figcaption>Brigadier General Harrison (left) with other commanders of the XX Corps, 1865</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1862, <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_United_States" title="President of the United States">President</a> <a href="/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln" title="Abraham Lincoln">Abraham Lincoln</a> issued a call for more recruits for the Union Army; Harrison wanted to enlist, but worried about how to support his young family.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200520_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200520-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> While visiting Governor <a href="/wiki/Oliver_Morton" class="mw-redirect" title="Oliver Morton">Oliver Morton</a>, Harrison found him distressed over the shortage of men answering the latest call. Harrison told the governor, "If I can be of any service, I will go."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace1888180Calhoun200534_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace1888180Calhoun200534-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Morton asked Harrison if he could help recruit a regiment, although he would not ask him to serve. Harrison recruited throughout northern Indiana to raise a regiment. Morton offered him the command, but Harrison declined, as he had no military experience. He was initially commissioned as a captain and company commander on July 22, 1862. Morton commissioned Harrison as a colonel on August 7, 1862, and the newly formed <a href="/wiki/70th_Indiana_Infantry_Regiment" title="70th Indiana Infantry Regiment">70th Indiana</a> was mustered into federal service on August 12, 1862. Once mustered, the regiment left Indiana to join the Union Army at Louisville, Kentucky.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace1888180–181Calhoun200521–23,_41,_44_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace1888180–181Calhoun200521–23,_41,_44-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Atlanta_campaign">Atlanta campaign</span></h3>
<p>For much of its first two years, the 70th Indiana performed reconnaissance duty and guarded railroads in <a href="/wiki/Kentucky" title="Kentucky">Kentucky</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tennessee" title="Tennessee">Tennessee</a>. In May 1864, Harrison and his regiment joined General <a href="/wiki/William_T._Sherman" class="mw-redirect" title="William T. Sherman">William T. Sherman</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Atlanta_Campaign" class="mw-redirect" title="Atlanta Campaign">Atlanta Campaign</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Army_of_the_Cumberland" title="Army of the Cumberland">Army of the Cumberland</a> and moved to the front lines.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> On January 2, 1864, Harrison was promoted to command the 1st Brigade of the 1st Division of the <a href="/wiki/XX_Corps_(Union_Army)" title="XX Corps (Union Army)">XX Corps</a>. He commanded the brigade at the battles of <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Resaca" title="Battle of Resaca">Resaca</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cassville,_Georgia" title="Cassville, Georgia">Cassville</a>, <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_New_Hope_Church" title="Battle of New Hope Church">New Hope Church</a>, Lost Mountain, <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Kennesaw_Mountain" title="Battle of Kennesaw Mountain">Kennesaw Mountain</a>, <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Marietta" title="Battle of Marietta">Marietta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Peachtree_Creek" title="Battle of Peachtree Creek">Peachtree Creek</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Atlanta" title="Battle of Atlanta">Atlanta</a>. When Sherman's main force began its <a href="/wiki/Sherman%27s_March_to_the_Sea" title="Sherman&#39;s March to the Sea">March to the Sea</a>, Harrison's brigade was transferred to the District of Etowah and participated in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Nashville" title="Battle of Nashville">Battle of Nashville</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200536–44Wallace1888209–225_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200536–44Wallace1888209–225-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> While encamped near Nashville, during a particularly cold winter, Harrison prepared coffee and brought it to his freezing men at night; his constant catchphrase as he took lead of his men was: "Come on, boys!" Harrison earned a reputation as a strong leader and as an officer who did not abandon his soldiers in battle.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Resaca">Resaca</span></h4>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Battle_of_Resaca--May_13_to_16,_1864.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Battle_of_Resaca--May_13_to_16%2C_1864.jpg/300px-Battle_of_Resaca--May_13_to_16%2C_1864.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="221" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Battle_of_Resaca--May_13_to_16%2C_1864.jpg/450px-Battle_of_Resaca--May_13_to_16%2C_1864.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Battle_of_Resaca--May_13_to_16%2C_1864.jpg/600px-Battle_of_Resaca--May_13_to_16%2C_1864.jpg 2x" data-file-width="948" data-file-height="697" /></a><figcaption>Battle of Resaca, in Georgia, where Harrison served under General <a href="/wiki/William_Tecumseh_Sherman" title="William Tecumseh Sherman">Sherman</a></figcaption></figure>  
<p>At the Battle of Resaca on May 15, 1864, Harrison faced Confederate Captain <a href="/wiki/Max_Corput" title="Max Corput">Max Van Den Corput’s</a> artillery battery, which occupied a position "some eighty yards in front of the main Confederate lines".<sup id="cite_ref-:0_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Sherman, renewing his assault on the center of the Confederate lines begun the previous day, was halted by Corput's four-gun, parapet-protected artillery battery; the battery was well placed to bedevil the Union ranks, and became "the center of a furious struggle".<sup id="cite_ref-:0_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Corput's artillery redoubt was highly fortified "with three infantry regiments in...rifle pits and four more regiments in the main trenches".<sup id="cite_ref-:2_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Harrison, leading the <a href="/wiki/70th_Indiana_Infantry_Regiment" title="70th Indiana Infantry Regiment">70th Indiana</a> Infantry Regiment, massed his troops in a ravine opposite Corput's position, along with the rest of Brigadier General Ward’s brigade.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Harrison and his regiment, leading the assault, then emerged from the ravine, advanced over the artillery parapet, overcame the Confederate gunners, and eliminated the threat. The battery was captured by hand-to-hand combat, and intense combat continued throughout the afternoon.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_43-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Harrison's unit, now exposed, found itself immediately subjected to intense gunfire from the main Confederate ranks and was forced to take cover.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_42-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Although no longer in Confederate hands, Corput's four 121-pound Napoleon Cannons<sup id="cite_ref-:2_43-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> sat in a "no man's land" for the rest of the day until nightfall, when Union soldiers "dug through the parapet, slipped ropes around the four cannons, and dragged them back to [their] lines".<sup id="cite_ref-:0_42-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Peachtree_Creek">Peachtree Creek</span></h4>
<p>During the Battle of Peachtree Creek, on July 20, 1864, Harrison commanded his brigade against General <a href="/wiki/Winfield_S._Featherston" title="Winfield S. Featherston">W. S. Featherston's</a> Mississippi Brigade, stopping the latter's "fierce assault" over Collier Road.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> At Peachtree Creek, Harrison's brigade comprised the <a href="/wiki/102nd_Illinois_Infantry_Regiment" title="102nd Illinois Infantry Regiment">102nd</a>, <a href="/wiki/105th_Illinois_Infantry_Regiment" title="105th Illinois Infantry Regiment">105th</a>, and <a href="/wiki/129th_Illinois_Infantry_Regiment" title="129th Illinois Infantry Regiment">129th Illinois Infantry Regiments</a>, the <a href="/wiki/79th_Ohio_Infantry_Regiment" title="79th Ohio Infantry Regiment">79th Ohio Infantry Regiment</a>, and his 70th Indiana Regiment; his brigade deployed in about the center of the Union line, engaging Maj. Gen. <a href="/wiki/William_Wing_Loring" title="William Wing Loring">William Wing Loring’s</a> Mississippi division and Alabama troops from General <a href="/wiki/Alexander_P._Stewart" title="Alexander P. Stewart">Alexander Stewart</a>'s corps.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> In his report after the battle Harrison wrote how "at one time during the fight," after his ammunition was dangerously depleted, he sent his acting assistant inspector-general Captain Scott and others to cut "cartridge-boxes from the rebel dead within our lines" and distribute them to his soldiers.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> According to Harrison's report, the losses from his brigade were "very slight" compared with those of Confederate forces; he thought this was because of battlefield topography, writing: "I believe, that the enemy, having the higher ground, fired too high."<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Harrison later supported the creation of an Atlanta National Military Park which would have included "substantial portions" of the Peachtree battlefield, writing in 1900: "The military incidents connected with the investment and ultimate capture of Atlanta are certainly worthy of commemoration and I should be glad to see the project succeed."<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Surrender_of_Atlanta_and_promotion">Surrender of Atlanta and promotion</span></h4>
<p>After the conclusion of the Atlanta Campaign on September 2, 1864, Harrison was among the initial Union forces to enter the surrendered city of Atlanta; General Sherman opined that Harrison served with "foresight, discipline and a fighting spirit".<sup id="cite_ref-:3_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Following the Atlanta Campaign, Harrison reported to Governor Morton in Indiana for special duty, and while there he campaigned for the position of Indiana’s Supreme Court Reporter and for President Lincoln's reelection; after the election he left for Georgia to join Sherman's <a href="/wiki/Sherman%27s_March_to_the_Sea" title="Sherman&#39;s March to the Sea">March to the Sea</a>, but instead was "given command of the 1st Brigade at Nashville."<sup id="cite_ref-:9_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Harrison led the brigade at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Nashville" title="Battle of Nashville">Battle of Nashville</a> in December, in a "decisive" action against the forces of General <a href="/wiki/John_Bell_Hood" title="John Bell Hood">John Bell Hood</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Notwithstanding his memorable military achievements and the praise he received for them, Harrison held a dim view of war; according to historian Allan B. Spetter he thought "war was a dirty business that no decent man would find pleasurable."<sup id="cite_ref-:3_49-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Later in 1888, the year he won the presidency, Harrison declared: "We Americans have no commission from God to police the world."<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Several weeks after the Battle of Nashville, Harrison "received orders to rejoin the 70th Indiana at Savannah, Georgia, after a brief furlough in Indianapolis"; however he caught scarlet fever and was delayed for a month, and then spent "several months training replacement troops in South Carolina."<sup id="cite_ref-:9_50-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>  
</p><p>On January 23, 1865, Lincoln nominated Harrison to the grade of <a href="/wiki/Brevet_(military)" title="Brevet (military)">brevet</a> <a href="/wiki/Brigadier_general_(United_States)" title="Brigadier general (United States)">brigadier general</a> of volunteers, to rank from that date, and the Senate confirmed the nomination on February 14, 1865.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Harrison was promoted because of his success at the battles of Resaca and Peachtree Creek.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Harrison finally returned to his old regiment the same day that news of President Lincoln’s assassination was received.<sup id="cite_ref-:9_50-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> He rode in the <a href="/wiki/Grand_Review_of_the_Armies" title="Grand Review of the Armies">Grand Review</a> in Washington, D.C. before mustering out with the 70th Indiana on June 8, 1865.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200536–44Wallace1888209–225_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200536–44Wallace1888209–225-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:9_50-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-war_career">Post-war career</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Indiana_politics">Indiana politics</span></h3>
<p>While serving in the Union Army in October 1864, Harrison was once again elected <a href="/wiki/Reporter_of_decisions" title="Reporter of decisions">reporter</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Indiana_Supreme_Court" title="Indiana Supreme Court">Indiana Supreme Court</a>, although he did not seek the position, and served as the Court's reporter for four more years. The position was not a politically powerful one, but it provided Harrison with a steady income for his work preparing and publishing court opinions, which he sold to the legal profession.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200519_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200519-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKinzer146–47_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinzer146–47-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> Harrison also resumed his law practice in Indianapolis. He became a skilled orator and known as "one of the state's leading lawyers".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1869 President <a href="/wiki/Ulysses_Simpson_Grant" class="mw-redirect" title="Ulysses Simpson Grant">Ulysses S. Grant</a> appointed Harrison to represent the federal government in a civil suit filed by <a href="/wiki/Lambdin_P._Milligan" title="Lambdin P. Milligan">Lambdin P. Milligan</a>, whose controversial wartime conviction for <a href="/wiki/Treason" title="Treason">treason</a> in 1864 led to the landmark <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_the_United_States" title="Supreme Court of the United States">U.S. Supreme Court</a> case <i><a href="/wiki/Ex_parte_Milligan" title="Ex parte Milligan">Ex parte Milligan</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBodenhamerBarrows1994444–445_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodenhamerBarrows1994444–445-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Case_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Case-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The civil case was referred to the U.S. Circuit Court for Indiana at Indianapolis, where it evolved into <i>Milligan v. Hovey</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-MilliganHovey_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MilliganHovey-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Although the jury found in Milligan's favor and he had sought hundreds of thousands of dollars in damages, state and federal statutes limited the amount the federal government had to award to Milligan to five dollars plus court costs.<sup id="cite_ref-MilliganHovey_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MilliganHovey-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200528_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200528-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace188893–94,_119_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace188893–94,_119-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Benjamin_Harrison_Home.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Benjamin_Harrison_Home.jpg/220px-Benjamin_Harrison_Home.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="158" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Benjamin_Harrison_Home.jpg/330px-Benjamin_Harrison_Home.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Benjamin_Harrison_Home.jpg/440px-Benjamin_Harrison_Home.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4204" data-file-height="3016" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison_Home" class="mw-redirect" title="Benjamin Harrison Home">Benjamin Harrison Home</a> in <a href="/wiki/Indianapolis,_Indiana" class="mw-redirect" title="Indianapolis, Indiana">Indianapolis, Indiana</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>With his increasing reputation, local Republicans urged Harrison to run for Congress. He initially confined his political activities to speaking on behalf of other Republican candidates, a task for which he received high praise from his colleagues.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200527–28Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200527–28Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> In 1872, Harrison campaigned for the Republican nomination for <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Indiana" title="Governor of Indiana">governor of Indiana</a>. Former governor Oliver Morton favored his opponent, <a href="/wiki/Thomas_M._Browne" title="Thomas M. Browne">Thomas M. Browne</a>, and Harrison lost his bid for statewide office.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200628_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200628-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> He returned to his law practice and, despite the <a href="/wiki/Panic_of_1873" title="Panic of 1873">Panic of 1873</a>, was financially successful enough to build a <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison_Home" class="mw-redirect" title="Benjamin Harrison Home">grand new home</a> in Indianapolis in 1874.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200529_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200529-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> He continued to make speeches on behalf of Republican candidates and policies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200530_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200530-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1876, when a scandal forced the original Republican nominee, <a href="/wiki/Godlove_Stein_Orth" title="Godlove Stein Orth">Godlove Stein Orth</a>, to drop out of the gubernatorial race, Harrison accepted the party's invitation to take his place on the ticket.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200532Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200532Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159Kinzer148_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159Kinzer148-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Harrison centered his campaign on economic policy and favored deflating the national currency. He was defeated in a plurality by <a href="/wiki/James_D._Williams" title="James D. Williams">James D. Williams</a>, losing by 5,084 votes out 434,457 cast,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace1888266Calhoun200532_&amp;_58_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace1888266Calhoun200532_&amp;_58-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> but Harrison built on his new prominence in state politics. When the <a href="/wiki/Great_Railroad_Strike_of_1877" title="Great Railroad Strike of 1877">Great Railroad Strike of 1877</a> reached Indianapolis, he gathered a citizen militia to make a show of support for owners and management,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFoner584_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner584-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> and helped to mediate an agreement between the workers and management and to prevent the strike from widening.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200533–34_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200533–34-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When United States Senator Morton died in 1877, the Republicans nominated Harrison to run for the seat, but the party failed to gain a majority in the state legislature, which at that time elected senators; the Democratic majority elected <a href="/wiki/Daniel_W._Voorhees" title="Daniel W. Voorhees">Daniel W. Voorhees</a> instead.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200535–36_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200535–36-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Seventeenth_Amendment_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Seventeenth_Amendment-73">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> In 1879, President <a href="/wiki/Rutherford_B._Hayes" title="Rutherford B. Hayes">Rutherford B. Hayes</a> appointed Harrison to the <a href="/wiki/Mississippi_River_Commission" class="mw-redirect" title="Mississippi River Commission">Mississippi River Commission</a>, which worked to develop internal improvements on the river.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> As a delegate to the <a href="/wiki/1880_Republican_National_Convention" title="1880 Republican National Convention">1880 Republican National Convention</a> the following year,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200536_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200536-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> he was instrumental in breaking a deadlock on candidates, and <a href="/wiki/James_A._Garfield" title="James A. Garfield">James A. Garfield</a> won the nomination.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="U.S._Senator_from_Indiana">U.S. Senator from Indiana</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Walter_Q._Gresham_-_Brady-Handy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Walter_Q._Gresham_-_Brady-Handy.jpg/170px-Walter_Q._Gresham_-_Brady-Handy.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Walter_Q._Gresham_-_Brady-Handy.jpg/255px-Walter_Q._Gresham_-_Brady-Handy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Walter_Q._Gresham_-_Brady-Handy.jpg/340px-Walter_Q._Gresham_-_Brady-Handy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3216" data-file-height="3848" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Walter_Q._Gresham" title="Walter Q. Gresham">Walter Q. Gresham</a>, Harrison's rival within the Indiana Republican Party</figcaption></figure>
<p>After Harrison led Indiana's Republican delegation at the 1880 Republican National Convention, he was considered the state's presumptive candidate for the U.S. Senate. He gave speeches in favor of Garfield in Indiana and New York, further raising his profile in the party. When the Republicans retook the majority in the <a href="/wiki/Indiana_General_Assembly" title="Indiana General Assembly">state legislature</a>, Harrison's election to a six-year term in the U.S. Senate was threatened by Judge <a href="/wiki/Walter_Q._Gresham" title="Walter Q. Gresham">Walter Q. Gresham</a>, his intraparty rival, but Harrison was ultimately chosen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200537_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200537-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> After Garfield's election as president in 1880, his administration offered Harrison a cabinet position, but Harrison declined in favor of continuing his service in the U.S. Senate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200560Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200560Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison served in the Senate from March 4, 1881, to March 3, 1887, and chaired the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Transportation_Routes_to_the_Seaboard" title="United States Senate Committee on Transportation Routes to the Seaboard">U.S. Senate Committee on Transportation Routes to the Seaboard</a> (<a href="/wiki/Forty-seventh_United_States_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="Forty-seventh United States Congress">47th Congress</a>) and the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Territories" class="mw-redirect" title="United States Senate Committee on Territories">U.S. Senate Committee on Territories</a> (<a href="/wiki/Forty-eighth_United_States_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="Forty-eighth United States Congress">48th</a> and <a href="/wiki/Forty-ninth_United_States_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="Forty-ninth United States Congress">49th Congresses</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace1888265–267Calhoun200559_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace1888265–267Calhoun200559-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1881, the major issue confronting Senator Harrison was the budget surplus. Democrats wanted to reduce the <a href="/wiki/Tariff" title="Tariff">tariff</a> and limit the amount of money the government took in; Republicans instead wanted to spend the money on <a href="/wiki/Internal_improvements" title="Internal improvements">internal improvements</a> and pensions for Civil War veterans. Harrison took his party's side and advocated for generous <a href="/wiki/Pension" title="Pension">pensions</a> for veterans and their widows.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200539_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200539-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> He also unsuccessfully supported aid for the education of Southerners, especially children of the freedmen; he believed that education was necessary to help the black population rise to political and economic equality with whites.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200539–40_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200539–40-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Harrison opposed the <a href="/wiki/Chinese_Exclusion_Act_(United_States)" class="mw-redirect" title="Chinese Exclusion Act (United States)">Chinese Exclusion Act of 1882</a>, which his party supported, because he thought it violated existing treaties with <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200540_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200540-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1884, Harrison and Gresham competed for influence at the <a href="/wiki/1884_Republican_National_Convention" title="1884 Republican National Convention">1884 Republican National Convention</a>; the delegation ended up supporting Senator <a href="/wiki/James_G._Blaine" title="James G. Blaine">James G. Blaine</a>, the eventual nominee.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200541–42_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200541–42-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/Mugwumps" title="Mugwumps">Mugwump</a> rebellion led by reform Republicans against Blaine's candidacy, Harrison at first stood aloof, "refusing to put his hat in the presidential ring," but after walking the middle ground he eventually supported Blaine "with energy and enthusiasm."<sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> In the Senate, Harrison achieved passage of his Dependent Pension Bill, only to see it vetoed by President <a href="/wiki/Grover_Cleveland" title="Grover Cleveland">Grover Cleveland</a>. His efforts to further the admission of new western states were stymied by Democrats, who feared that the new states would elect Republicans to Congress.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200542_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200542-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In 1885 the Democrats <a href="/wiki/Redistricting" title="Redistricting">redistricted</a> the Indiana state legislature, which resulted in an increased Democratic majority in 1886, despite an overall Republican majority statewide.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200543–44_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200543–44-85">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> In 1887, largely as a result of the Democratic <a href="/wiki/Gerrymandering" title="Gerrymandering">gerrymandering</a> of Indiana's legislative districts, Harrison was defeated in his bid for reelection.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159_26-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGugin_and_St._Clair159-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Following a deadlock in the <a href="/wiki/Indiana_Senate" title="Indiana Senate">state senate</a>, the state legislature eventually chose Democrat <a href="/wiki/David_Turpie" title="David Turpie">David Turpie</a> as Harrison's successor in the Senate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200666_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMooreHale200666-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Harrison returned to Indianapolis and resumed his law practice, but stayed active in state and national politics.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200545–46_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200545–46-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> A year after his senatorial defeat, Harrison declared his candidacy for the Republican nomination; he dubbed himself a "living and rejuvenated Republican," a reference to his lack of a power base.<sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Thereafter, the phrase "'Rejuvenated Republicanism' became the slogan of his presidential campaign."<sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Election_of_1888">Election of 1888</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1888_United_States_presidential_election" title="1888 United States presidential election">1888 United States presidential election</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nomination_for_president">Nomination for president</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1888_Republican_National_Convention" title="1888 Republican National Convention">1888 Republican National Convention</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Harrison-Morton_1888.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Harrison-Morton_1888.jpg/220px-Harrison-Morton_1888.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Harrison-Morton_1888.jpg/330px-Harrison-Morton_1888.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Harrison-Morton_1888.jpg/440px-Harrison-Morton_1888.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="500" /></a><figcaption>Harrison–Morton campaign poster</figcaption></figure>
<p>The initial favorite for the Republican nomination was the previous nominee, James G. Blaine of <a href="/wiki/Maine" title="Maine">Maine</a>. After his narrow defeat against Cleveland in 1884 Blaine became the front-runner for 1888, but decided to remove his name from contention.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> After Blaine wrote several letters denying any interest in the nomination, his supporters divided among other candidates, with Senator <a href="/wiki/John_Sherman_(politician)" class="mw-redirect" title="John Sherman (politician)">John Sherman</a> of <a href="/wiki/Ohio" title="Ohio">Ohio</a> as the leader among them.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200547_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200547-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Others, including <a href="/wiki/Chauncey_Depew" title="Chauncey Depew">Chauncey Depew</a> of <a href="/wiki/New_York_(state)" title="New York (state)">New York</a>, <a href="/wiki/Russell_Alger" class="mw-redirect" title="Russell Alger">Russell Alger</a> of <a href="/wiki/Michigan" title="Michigan">Michigan</a>, and Harrison's old nemesis Walter Q. Gresham—now <a href="/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Seventh_Circuit" title="United States Court of Appeals for the Seventh Circuit">a federal appellate court judge in Chicago</a>—also sought the delegates' support at the <a href="/wiki/1888_Republican_National_Convention" title="1888 Republican National Convention">1888 Republican National Convention</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200547_89-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200547-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Harrison "marshaled his troops" to stop Gresham from gaining control of the Indiana delegation while simultaneously presenting himself "as an attractive alternative to Blaine."<sup id="cite_ref-:5_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Blaine did not publicly endorse any of the candidates, but on March 1, 1888, he privately wrote that "the one man remaining who in my judgment can make the best one is Benjamin Harrison."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter8-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Later at the National Convention, which took place in June, Blaine "threw his support to Harrison in the hope of uniting the party" against President Cleveland; nonetheless, the nomination fight that followed was "hotly contested."<sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The convention opened on June 19 at the <a href="/wiki/Auditorium_Building_(Chicago)" class="mw-redirect" title="Auditorium Building (Chicago)">Auditorium Building</a> in Chicago, Illinois.<sup id="cite_ref-:6_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Proceedings began with an announcement of the party platform; Lincoln was extolled as the "first great leader" of the Republican Party and an "immortal champion of liberty and the rights of the people."<sup id="cite_ref-:7_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Republican presidents Grant, Garfield, and Arthur were likewise acknowledged with "remembrance and gratitude." The "fundamental idea of the Republican party" was declared to be "hostility to all forms of despotism and oppression," and the Brazilian people were congratulated for their <a href="/wiki/Abolition_of_slavery_in_Brazil" class="mw-redirect" title="Abolition of slavery in Brazil">recent abolition of slavery</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:7_91-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The convention alleged that the "present Administration and the Democratic majority in Congress owe their existence to the suppression of the ballot by a criminal nullification of the Constitution." Anticipating a principal part of Harrison's campaign, the convention also declared itself "uncompromisingly in favor of the <a href="/wiki/American_System_(economic_plan)" title="American System (economic plan)">American system of protection</a>," and protested "against its destruction as proposed by the President and his party."<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> The tariff was later to become the "main issue of the campaign" in 1888.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_88-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> The admission of six new states during Harrison's term, between 1889 and 1890, was anticipated with the declaration: "whenever the conditions of population, material resources...and morality are such as to insure a stable local government," the people "should be permitted...to form for themselves constitutions and State government, and be admitted into the Union."<sup id="cite_ref-:7_91-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The convention insisted that "The pending bills in the Senate to enable the people of Washington, North Dakota and Montana Territories to...establish State governments, should be passed without unnecessary delay."<sup id="cite_ref-:7_91-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The convention began with seventeen candidates for the nomination.<sup id="cite_ref-:6_90-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison placed fifth on the first ballot, with Senator Sherman in the lead, and the next few ballots showed little change.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200550_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200550-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> As the convention moved forward, Harrison became "everyone's second choice in a field of seven candidates."<sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Then, after Sherman "faltered in the balloting,"<sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Harrison gained support. Blaine supporters shifted their support among candidates they found acceptable, and when they shifted to Harrison, they found a candidate who could attract the votes of many other delegations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200551–52_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200551–52-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> Intending to make it undeniably clear he would not be a candidate, Blaine left the country and was staying with <a href="/wiki/Andrew_Carnegie" title="Andrew Carnegie">Andrew Carnegie</a> in Scotland when the National Convention began, and did not return to the United States until August; the delegates finally accepted Blaine's refusal to be nominated.<sup id="cite_ref-:8_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> After New York switched to Harrison's column, he gained the needed momentum for victory.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_88-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Harrison was nominated as the party's presidential candidate on the eighth ballot, by a count of 544 to 108 votes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWallace1888271_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWallace1888271-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Levi_P._Morton" title="Levi P. Morton">Levi P. Morton</a> of New York—a banker, former U.S. Minister to France, and former U.S. congressman—was chosen as his running mate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter9_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter9-97">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> At their <a href="/wiki/1888_Democratic_National_Convention" title="1888 Democratic National Convention">National Convention in St. Louis</a>, Democrats rallied behind President Cleveland and his running-mate, Senator <a href="/wiki/Allen_G._Thurman" title="Allen G. Thurman">Allen G. Thurman</a> from Ohio; Vice President <a href="/wiki/Thomas_A._Hendricks" title="Thomas A. Hendricks">Hendricks</a> had died in office on November 25, 1885.<sup id="cite_ref-:4_83-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> After returning to America, Blaine visited Harrison at his home in October.<sup id="cite_ref-:8_95-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Campaign_against_Cleveland">Campaign against Cleveland</span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{display:flex;flex-direction:row;clear:left;flex-wrap:wrap;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{margin:1px;float:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-left{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-right{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-center{text-align:center}@media all and (max-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbinner{width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:none!important;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{justify-content:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{float:none!important;max-width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:331px;max-width:331px"><div class="trow"><div class="theader" style="text-align:center;background-color:">Harrison campaign paraphernalia</div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:235px;max-width:235px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:BENJAMIN_HARRISON_1888_CAMPAIGN_HANDKERCHIEF.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/cb/BENJAMIN_HARRISON_1888_CAMPAIGN_HANDKERCHIEF.jpg/233px-BENJAMIN_HARRISON_1888_CAMPAIGN_HANDKERCHIEF.jpg" decoding="async" width="233" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/cb/BENJAMIN_HARRISON_1888_CAMPAIGN_HANDKERCHIEF.jpg/350px-BENJAMIN_HARRISON_1888_CAMPAIGN_HANDKERCHIEF.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/cb/BENJAMIN_HARRISON_1888_CAMPAIGN_HANDKERCHIEF.jpg/466px-BENJAMIN_HARRISON_1888_CAMPAIGN_HANDKERCHIEF.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1422" data-file-height="1237" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">1888 Harrison campaign handkerchief</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:92px;max-width:92px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Benjamin_Harrison_Campaign_Ribbon_with_Photo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/be/Benjamin_Harrison_Campaign_Ribbon_with_Photo.jpg/90px-Benjamin_Harrison_Campaign_Ribbon_with_Photo.jpg" decoding="async" width="90" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/be/Benjamin_Harrison_Campaign_Ribbon_with_Photo.jpg/135px-Benjamin_Harrison_Campaign_Ribbon_with_Photo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/be/Benjamin_Harrison_Campaign_Ribbon_with_Photo.jpg/180px-Benjamin_Harrison_Campaign_Ribbon_with_Photo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="2252" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">Ribbon with photo</div></div></div></div></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:ElectoralCollege1888.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/ElectoralCollege1888.svg/220px-ElectoralCollege1888.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="128" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/ElectoralCollege1888.svg/330px-ElectoralCollege1888.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/ElectoralCollege1888.svg/440px-ElectoralCollege1888.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1020" data-file-height="593" /></a><figcaption>Results of the 1888 election</figcaption></figure>
<p>Harrison's opponent in the general election was incumbent President Grover Cleveland. Harrison reprised a more traditional <a href="/wiki/Front_porch_campaign" title="Front porch campaign">front-porch campaign</a>, abandoned by his immediate predecessors; he received visiting delegations to Indianapolis and made over 90 pronouncements from his hometown.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter11_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter11-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> The Republicans campaigned heavily in favor of <a href="/wiki/Tariff" title="Tariff">protective tariffs</a>, turning out protectionist voters in the important industrial states of the North. The election took place on Tuesday, November 6, 1888; it focused on the swing states of New York, New Jersey, Connecticut, and Harrison's home state of Indiana.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter10_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter10-99">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> Harrison and Cleveland split the four, with Harrison winning New York and Indiana.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200543Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter13_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200543Socolofsky_&amp;_Spetter13-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Voter turnout was 79.3%, reflecting large interest in the campaign; nearly eleven million votes were cast.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200557_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200557-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Harrison received 90,000 fewer popular votes than Cleveland, but carried the <a href="/wiki/Electoral_College_(United_States)" class="mw-redirect" title="Electoral College (United States)">Electoral College</a> 233 to 168.<sup id="cite_ref-EV_1888_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EV_1888-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> Allegations were made against Republicans for engaging in irregular ballot practices; an example was described as <a href="/wiki/Blocks_of_Five" class="mw-redirect" title="Blocks of Five">Blocks of Five</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter13Williams191–192_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter13Williams191–192-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> On October 31 the <i>Indiana Sentinel</i> published a letter allegedly by Harrison's friend and supporter, <a href="/wiki/William_Wade_Dudley" title="William Wade Dudley">William Wade Dudley</a>, offering to bribe voters in "blocks of five" to ensure Harrison's election. Harrison neither defended nor repudiated Dudley, but allowed him to remain on the campaign for the remaining few days. After the election, Harrison never spoke to Dudley again.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliams191–192_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams191–192-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harrison had made no political bargains, but his supporters had made many pledges on his behalf. When Boss <a href="/wiki/Matthew_Quay" title="Matthew Quay">Matthew Quay</a> of Pennsylvania, who was rebuffed for a Cabinet position for his political support during the convention, heard that Harrison ascribed his narrow victory to <a href="/wiki/Divine_Providence" class="mw-redirect" title="Divine Providence">Providence</a>, Quay exclaimed that Harrison would never know "how close a number of men were compelled to approach...the penitentiary to make him president".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200555,_60_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200555,_60-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Harrison was known as the Centennial President because his inauguration celebrated the <a href="/wiki/George_Washington%27s_legacy#Centennial_celebration" class="mw-redirect" title="George Washington&#39;s legacy">centenary of the first inauguration</a> of <a href="/wiki/George_Washington" title="George Washington">George Washington</a> in 1789.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECalhoun200547–54_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECalhoun200547–54-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> In the congressional elections, Republicans increased their membership in the House of Representatives by 19 seats.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter14_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESocolofsky_&amp;_Spetter14-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span id="Presidency_.281889.E2.80.931893.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Presidency_(1889–1893)">Presidency (1889–1893)</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Presidency_of_Benjamin_Harrison" title="Presidency of Benjamin Harrison">Presidency of Benjamin Harrison</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Inauguration_and_cabinet">Inauguration and cabinet</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright">
<div class="thumbinner" style="width: 232px;">
<div class="thumbimage" style="width: 230px; height: 270px; overflow: hidden;">
<div style="position: relative; top: -50px; left: -50px; width: 325px"><div class="noresize"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:HARRISON,_Benjamin-President_(BEP_engraved_portrait).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="BEP engraved portrait of Harrison as President" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/HARRISON%2C_Benjamin-President_%28BEP_engraved_portrait%29.jpg/325px-HARRISON%2C_Benjamin-President_%28BEP_engraved_portrait%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="325" height="412" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/HARRISON%2C_Benjamin-President_%28BEP_engraved_portrait%29.jpg/488px-HARRISON%2C_Benjamin-President_%28BEP_engraved_portrait%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/HARRISON%2C_Benjamin-President_%28BEP_engraved_portrait%29.jpg/650px-HARRISON%2C_Benjamin-President_%28BEP_engraved_portrait%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="9000" data-file-height="11400" /></a></span></div></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:HARRISON,_Benjamin-President_(BEP_engraved_portrait).jpg" title="File:HARRISON, Benjamin-President (BEP engraved portrait).jpg"> </a></div><a href="/wiki/Bureau_of_Engraving_and_Printing" title="Bureau of Engraving and Printing">BEP</a> engraved portrait of Harrison as President
</div>
</div>
</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:200px;max-width:200px"><div class="trow"><div class="theader" style="text-align:center;background-color:">1889 inaugural ribbons</div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:92px;max-width:92px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:ORNATE_HARRISON_1889_INAUGURAL_RIBBON.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9a/ORNATE_HARRISON_1889_INAUGURAL_RIBBON.jpg/90px-ORNATE_HARRISON_1889_INAUGURAL_RIBBON.jpg" decoding="async" width="90" height="191" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9a/ORNATE_HARRISON_1889_INAUGURAL_RIBBON.jpg/135px-ORNATE_HARRISON_1889_INAUGURAL_RIBBON.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9a/ORNATE_HARRISON_1889_INAUGURAL_RIBBON.jpg/180px-ORNATE_HARRISON_1889_INAUGURAL_RIBBON.jpg 2x" data-file-width="407" data-file-height="864" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">New York Republican Assn. ribbon</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:104px;max-width:104px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a
